Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

21 Thanksgiving Table Scripts For The Relative Who Always Starts Sh*t

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

“I didn’t know people I knew held these types of views” is a zinger.

It happens every year but it never gets less jarring. That one drunk uncle brings up politics (and never in a thoughtful way). Your mother-in-law asks about that one sensitive subject that’s really none of her business. Your grandma comments on how much food is on your teen daughter’s plate. Whatever the topic, family gatherings have a way of bringing a bunch of people with differing opinions around one table, where they’re just comfortable enough with everyone to spew their hot takes and outdated perspectives. If you have someone like this in your family, here are some Thanksgiving table scripts to respond to that one relative who always starts sh*t.

There’s nothing worse than when that one pot-stirring family member crosses you and you immediately feel your temper flare red hot. Or you get completely flustered and fumble for words. When someone pushes a boundary of yours, take a beat before responding.

“Pause, take a breath, and press your feet into the ground to feel a bit more grounded. Give yourself permission to slow down and stay in your body so you don't feel pressured to keep answering or discussing something you're not comfortable with. You don't owe anyone at the dinner table answers you aren't comfortable giving,” says Chloë Bean, LMFT, a somatic trauma therapist in Los Angeles.

You’re not alone in feeling like the holidays are a hotbed for family conflict, experts say.

“Perhaps things start off great, but after a few rounds of seasonal cocktails, Uncle So-and-So and Grandma Such-and-Such start spouting off some out-of-pocket sh*t. This has only increased as our socio-political climate intensifies and the gap between generational understandings widens,” says Whitney Polich, LICSW, licensed clinical social worker. “Despite this happening every year, perhaps we are still a bit gobsmacked and unsure of what to say, until the drive home when the perfect clapback comes to mind.”

Whether you clap back in the moment and escalate or would rather de-escalate, practicing a few responses in your head (or even in the mirror) ahead of the gathering can help you stay grounded and protect your peace.

There are three genres of response you could go with here, says Ashley Hamm, LPC, a licensed professional counselor who specializes in anxiety and people-pleasing.

“Sometimes acting surprised or baffled is enough to throw someone off-kilter and get them off the subject. This is especially useful if someone is sharing something problematic that they might know deep down is inappropriate to share,” says Hamm.

Try these: “I’m surprised you feel comfortable sharing ideas like that.” “I have no idea what you are talking about.” “I’m surprised to hear you say that; I didn’t know people I knew held these types of views.”

Keep a few alternate subjects in the back of your mind in case you need a quick diversion — a conversational smoke bomb, if you will.

Think: “Speaking of the news, I saw a really funny story about…” “Speaking of the news, I saw a really heartwarming story about…” “I’d rather not talk about our opinions on that because I don’t get to see you very often, and I’d rather hear about what you’ve been up to personally!”

“Simply acting like you didn’t hear them is a great strategy to stay disentangled from sensitive or divisive topics. Using some of the changing topics ideas above, you can simply start a new topic, or respond to the most recent unproblematic thing someone said,” Hamm says.

For example: “Oh, Aunt Mary, you were just talking about your recipe for the green bean casserole. I’d love to hear more about where you found it.” “Hmm, not sure what to say to that. Anyways, I’ve been trying to watch more movies lately. Has anyone seen anything good?”

Here are even more replies to consider from Polich and Bean:

Bookmark ‘em for your family’s Thanksgiving and thank yourself later.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Magma and a possible environment for ancient life might be present on Mars in the region of a large volcano on the Red Planet.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A noxious gas found seeping through cracks on Mars hints that there may be magma flowing beneath the Red Planet's surface — and perhaps even an environment similar to habitable ones on Earth, a new study suggests.

Europe's ExoMars Trace Gas Orbiter (TGO) spacecraft spotted a possible signal of hydrogen chloride gas leaking from rifts in the region of Alba Mons, the largest volcano on Mars in terms of surface area. (Mars' famous Olympus Mons is the largest volcano in terms of height.) Scientists interpret this gas as emissions from hot magma below the regolith of Mars.

Although more research is required to confirm the emissions, the team suggested that Alba Mons may host an environment similar to hydrothermal vents on Earth, where life once likely arose and continues to thrive today.

The study, published in the journal Science Advances Wednesday (Nov. 4), is not the first to suggest that magma may be flowing on Mars; NASA's InSight lander interpreted its presence through seismic observations and modeling earlier this year. But the new findings are the strongest evidence yet of gases being released by dormant volcanoes on Mars, first study author Alexander Trokhimovskiy, at Russia's Space Research Institute, told Live Science.

"Our study offers a complementary and potentially independent line of evidence [of Martian magma]: Atmospheric observations that reveal a possible signature of gas release from a subsurface magmatic system," Trokhimovskiy wrote in an email.

TGO first detected hydrogen chloride gas in Mars' atmosphere in 2021, but scientists were hesitant to link it to volcanic activity. The gas appeared to be seasonal, its distribution was not localized at a possible volcanic region, and other signs of volcanic gases, like sulfur, were not present, study co-author Kevin Olson, a visiting researcher at the University of Oxford who studies Mars gases, told Live Science in a separate email.

A photo of Mars with the Tharsis region centered.

Further work, however, suggested that the gas wasn't just popping up in the warmer spring and summer in Mars' southern hemisphere; it also appeared in the winter in the northern hemisphere, he noted.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

"I managed to catch two observations from the start of northern winter in the first Mars year of our mission, and a single observation the next year," Olson said, adding that a year on Mars is closer to two years on Earth due to the planet's wider orbit. "These out-of-season measurements were close to one another in location and time in both years, which was rather surprising. We then set about creating a campaign of special, targeted measurements at this time and place."

That campaign lasted two Mars years — roughly four Earth years — and increased the likelihood that scientists are seeing "an injection" of hydrogen chloride into the atmosphere from volcano-adjacent vents, Olson said.

The scientists confirmed the unseasonal observations in the northern hemisphere, finding a "distinct and pronounced signal" that then diminishes and falls below the spacecraft's detection threshold. "This inconsistency is more suggestive of a puff of gas entering the atmosphere than of regular atmospheric chemistry," he said. "While the link remains a hypothesis, we have now seen this gas appear locally, and nowhere else, over four Mars years of observations."

Daniel Mège, also a co-author of the paper and a professor who studies planetary geology at the Polish Academy of Sciences, identified the fissures where the gas may be emerging. He said the complementary evidence from InSight (on the ground) and TGO (circling above) helps give the study weight: InSight spotted activity in the crust, while TGO detected the emissions in the atmosphere.

Although magma activity is not in itself a sign of life, Mège noted that Earth's first life-forms came from sites like hydrothermal zones, which have a similar confluence of materials and energy as the potential vents on Mars. But conditions would need to be favorable in that area for at least millions of years to allow life to form, he cautioned.

"If these requirements are met at local scale, at the scale of the seepage structures, then living organisms might wait for us to discover them," he told Live Science in an email. "But maintaining stable conditions over such a long period at a very local scale is not obvious."

What do you know about the Red Planet? Test your knowledge with our Mars quiz!

Elizabeth Howell was staff reporter at Space.com between 2022 and 2024 and a regular contributor to Live Science and Space.com between 2012 and 2022. Elizabeth's reporting includes multiple exclusives with the White House, speaking several times with the International Space Station, witnessing five human spaceflight launches on two continents, flying parabolic, working inside a spacesuit, and participating in a simulated Mars mission. Her latest book, "Why Am I Taller?" (ECW Press, 2022) is co-written with astronaut Dave Williams.

US midterm tsunami sweeps away video game composer who talked about ‘double booty’ and made sure to never touch anyone, just loom’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Marty O’Donnell’s bid for Nevada’s 3rd Congressional District ended in disappointment—for Marty—as he’s one of many Republican candidates who got swept away by a massive blue wave that kept Democratic incumbent Susie Lee’s seat after a 20-percent margin of victory.

You may not recognize Marty’s name, but chances are you’re familiar with his work. He served as Bungie’s main composer for over two decades, responsible for Destiny‘s soundtrack and, more importantly, Halo’s hauntingly beautiful main theme. Unfortunately, this really is a prime candidate for separating the art from the artist.

The first possibility is that Marty had received a glowing endorsement from Donald Trump shortly before the election.

It is my Great Honor to endorse America First Patriot, Marty O'Donnell, who is running to represent the incredible people of Nevada’s 3rd Congressional District. Marty is running against Susie Lee, a true Radical Left Extremist who does not represent the Values of Southern…

Unfortunately for O’Donnell, he was gunning for Nevada, a district that was greatly impacted by Trump’s unwavering stance on tariffs, so maybe that’s not the guy you want to seem very well connected to at that point. Still, there are other equally important reasons to point out, like the fact that O’Donnell is reportedly not someone you want close, or even “looming” around, especially if you’re a woman.

A few of his ex-colleagues have talked about prior experiences, none of them good. The scariest part, however, is that Marty might already be so familiar with these situations that he’s already devised a game plan.

When talking about a particularly infamous event at a Bungie Halloween party, Marty said, “Yes, the women got really creeped out but I made sure to never touch anyone, just loom.”

Oh, and that’s all after he’d become a pioneer in appropriating Halo imagery to bastardize it with AI to create anti-immigration propaganda.

After her tremendous victory, Susie Lee likely destroyed any horcruxes Marty likely still had lying around by tagging him in a tweet showing a viral video where a bunch of school kids perform a surprisingly beautiful rendition of the Halo theme inside a bathroom. The video fades to black with the message “the fight is finished.”

Enjoy your retirement @MartyTheElder pic.twitter.com/vH8KwKWrdl

I usually prefer when winners celebrate with grace and avoid teabagging their enemies, but Susie and so many others have been through so much crap because of “MartyTheElder” that I just cannot condemn anyone for double-tapping the guy.

As for Marty himself, well, he’d previously stated that he never really wanted to be a politician in the first place, so, if we’re to take his words for it, then everyone’s all the better for this result. Yes, even the people who cannot stop talking about how you shouldn’t be mixing politics with video games.

Democrats Eye Tougher Data Center Regulations as They Regain Control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats who rode data center discontent to victory in the midterms are expected to pursue strict new regulations on data centers when they take control of Congress in January.

The Democratic sweep flipped dozens of House and Senate seats, and a number of those races hinged on broadsides against Republicans for their purported support of the industry. Meanwhile, voters considered more than 40 local measures to constrain data center development, and almost all of them passed.

The results are likely to bring new life into Democrats' efforts to add guardrails to the artificial intelligence buildout. But their majority does not go far enough to override a veto from President Donald Trump, who has sought to aggressively promote data centers in the United States, in the pursuit of what he dubs "super intelligence."

Polls show broad concerns that the pace of data center construction could drive up power costs, raise land prices, and create environmental problems. Recent Realtor.com® research on data centers found they're increasingly moving into rural areas.

"If the Democrats take control of Congress, it could be a major problem for data center developers," Balaji Tammabattula, CEO of BaRupOn LLC, which develops energy infrastructure for data center companies, recently told Realtor.com.

Data center companies, he said, are already reconsidering future investments. In Texas, Senate winner James Talarico released a blueprint for data center regulation with more enforcement. The state has a temporary moratorium on data centers in place, which Democratic Gov.-Elect Gina Hinojosa has pledged to extend.

Just a few hours before polls closed, The Hill reported that Microsoft, Amazon, Meta, Google, and other tech companies were considering a more formal framework for data center development.

"Data centers in space looking pretty appealing right now," Elon Musk posted on X shortly after the results came in.

Congress' one effort to rein in the impacts of data centers fell short just before the midterms. Several weeks ago, the House passed a bill to encourage utilities to shield ratepayers from the costs associated with data center buildouts, the Ratepayer Protection Act.

That followed widespread backlash against data center buildouts. But Senate Democrats tanked the bill because it didn't go far enough. They've coalesced around a few competing ideas for how to regulate data centers at the federal level.

In the meantime, Democrats put forth their own bill, the Guaranteeing Rate Insulation from Data Centers (GRID) Act, with more enforcement power and penalties. Among its powers, it would require data center developers to provide their own energy, so they don't pull from the local power grid. It would also require more disclosure about data center developments and give the government the power to issue fines.

Also, 20 Democrats signed on to a bill that would create a total moratorium on data center development. But the president has veto power, and he has opposed such a harsh move.

Trump has advocated for data center development in the United States to prevent the technology being developed abroad. In the face of public backlash, he convened technology leaders in Washington in September and asked them to self-police the build-out of super intelligence.

The White House also released an accord signed by those major companies committing to developing the technology safely. Trump called it "morally binding."

While data centers affect property taxes, the exact impact on homeowners' tax burden is complicated. And the study found no impact on property values from large facilities in the two years after they open.

Democrats may find an opening to create more rules around data center construction. Although Democrats also took control of governor's offices in many major states with data center developments, Realtor.com senior economist Jake Krimmel says that doesn't necessarily mean they will lead to sharp constraints on data centers.

"Utilities costs, rather than home values, are probably the biggest immediate change voters are feeling or fearing," Krimmel says. "Our analysis of data center impacts on the housing market found essentially no effect on inventory or sales behavior so far."

The Urban Institute suggested a few levers to help data centers support housing affordability. It suggested tax relief or energy cost offsets for communities that host many data centers. Otherwise, they risk straining local housing markets due to rising electricity costs, especially in high-growth regions.

Meanwhile, Brookings suggested an electricity tax on data centers or some kind of offset to local governments. But it warned that regulation of the sector could encourage developers to build more centers overseas.

It's unlikely the currently divided Congress will use the remainder of its term to pass rules around data centers. But when the new one comes in, it can reintroduce the GRID Act or other bills. The GRID Act’s 20-megawatt threshold would cover essentially the entire pipeline, more than 99% of planned capacity, Krimmel says.

"That would give homeowners the most direct protection on rates," Krimmel explains. "Requiring new projects to bring their own power could also slow data center development, and the jury is also still out on the local economic and labor market impacts of data centers on localities."

Tristan Navera is a senior reporter on housing policy, covering trends and solutions in the housing market from Washington, DC. He was previously a senior reporter at Bloomberg Law, and before that covered real estate for the Washington Business Journal. Earlier in his career, he spent a decade reporting on business and real estate in Dayton and Columbus, OH. A Cincinnati native, he holds a journalism degree from Ohio University.

Voters across the country opted for nearly every local ballot measure that would curb the rate of data center expansion.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On Tuesday, tens of millions of Americans voted in midterm elections to deliver a sweep of generational Democratic victories in federal and state races, reflecting a leftward electoral shift that's being interpreted as a nationwide rejection of Trumpism.

But even as Republican candidates and incumbents suffered defeats in what had traditionally been considered some of the party's most entrenched strongholds, the midterms also issued a stinging rebuke to another major source of voter frustration: Data centers. At nearly every opportunity, Americans voted in favor of local ballot measures to halt, slow, or stymie the pace of data center construction in their communities.

As tracked by Data Centers on the Ballot, across the 47 local midterm elections in the American cities and townships that featured ballot measures on data center construction, voters favored restrictions on the rate of data center expansion in 38 of the 39 completed counts. Of the eight races that remain open, voters supporting additional data center restrictions are leading in all six of the races with only partial reporting. For the two remaining races, no results have yet been posted.

Here's the rundown of the types of measures voters are supporting:

At time of writing, only one ballot measure that would have introduced data center restrictions failed: In Harwood, North Dakota, voters refused an amendment to the city charter that would have required Harwood to commission an independent review before approving new data center construction or expansion.

Aside from that one exception, it's not hard to suss out why so many of those measures have passed. NBC exit polls show that 56% of yesterday's midterm voters considered AI data centers either "crucial" or "a major factor" in their choice of Congress person, with data center opponents driven by concerns of environmental toll, energy costs, noise pollution, and—one must imagine—the upward-spiraling cost of RAM.

Channing Tatum says he's still fighting for his standalone Gambit movie... despite Disney pulling the plug back in 2019.

"We had three different directors that were signed up to do it, and the first one, the studio, Fox, they had a fallout, so that one didn't work out," Tatum tells ScreenRant. "The second one was just in development for so long with somebody that I was just like, 'We got to move to making this thing.' So we separated ourselves, and then the third one almost got going with Gore Verbinski, and then creatively all three of us, the studio, him and me, we started in the same place, and then as it developed, we just all grew apart."

Tatum went on to say that Shawn Levy was interested in directing a previous script, but that he and his producing partner Reid Carolin just weren't ready yet (and keep in mind the year was around 2014). The actor says he's still "fighting" for the chance to make the film, and that he's waiting for a "very, very good director to realize it."

A standalone Gambit movie starring Tatum was greenlit in 2014 and ultimately cancelled in 2019 when Disney acquired Fox. Tatum's featured appearance as the card-wielding X-Men member in Deadpool and Wolverine made me feel warm and fuzzy inside, as I could only imagine how stoked Tatum must've been to finally put on the suit.

E.l.f. Beauty posts 51% sales increase, raises full-year guidance

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

E.l.f. Beauty on Wednesday blew past Wall Street expectations as it posted a 51% increase in net sales in its second fiscal quarter.

The beauty company said it saw its 31st consecutive quarter of net sales growth, attributing the streak to strong performance across its portfolio. The revenue spike was driven by both retail and e-commerce channels across the U.S. and internationally, the company added.

"The thing I'm most pleased about is we saw strength across our entire portfolio," CEO Tarang Amin told CNBC in an interview. "Every one of our brands contributed strong growth this quarter."

Amin added that E.l.f. is the most-searched brand on TikTok Shop and the No. 2 brand in cosmetics on the platform. The company also said it saw strong results for Rhode, the skincare brand it acquired last year.

The company raised its full fiscal-year guidance following the better-than-anticipated quarter. It now expects net sales in the range of $2 billion to $2.03 billion, compared with a previous range of between $1.94 billion and $1.97 billion. It also upped its adjusted EBITDA projection to a range of $421 million to $427 million from between $401 million and $407 million.

E.l.f. said it now expects adjusted earnings per share for the year to come in between $3.82 and $3.86, exceeding prior guidance of between $3.50 and $3.55 per share. It's also expecting a net income range of $231 million to $233 million, up from a previous range of $212 million to $215 million.

It had already raised its guidance last quarter after outperforming its expectations.

Amin said the company is "very bullish" on the holiday season, expecting high sales through the end of the year.

Here's how the company performed in its second fiscal quarter compared with what Wall Street was expecting, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

For the period ended Sept. 30, E.l.f. reported net income of $30.03 million, or 50 cents per share, up significantly from roughly $3 million, or 5 cents per share, the year prior.

E.l.f. also saw significant growth in its international channels, with business up 165%. Amin told CNBC that only 25% of the company's business is outside of the U.S. and that it has a "tremendous opportunity" to build on that momentum.

The company also said its gross margin increased 1.6% during the quarter, including a 0.3% benefit from tariff refunds. Amin previously said the retailer planned to fully reinvest that money into pricing and marketing to continue driving growth.

Though E.l.f. received most of its refunds in its first fiscal quarter, some of those funds flowed through the second quarter, and the company expects to receive a yearly total of around $55 million. Amin told CNBC on Wednesday that the company lowered the price of about 10% of its units by $1 because of the tariff benefits.

"We're seeing great unit momentum based on that," he said.

‘We Have No Shared Reality’: Two Political Influencers on the Future of Politics Online

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A voter going to the polls Tuesday night was more likely than not to use information from their favorite influencer than traditional news when casting their vote. According to a recent YouGov survey, nearly 65 percent of 2,000 adults surveyed say they receive their political information from digital creators.

Given that that statistic jumps to 77 percent when you isolate Gen Z, it’s unlikely to change anytime soon. On the heels of a historic Democratic wave capturing the House and Senate Tuesday, Kat Abughazaleh, who ran for an Illinois House seat earlier this year as a Democrat, and Republican strategist CJ Pearson talked about the future of influencers and algorithms at the WIRED World Fair in Miami on Wednesday.

The Trump administration has been particularly aggressive in welcoming right-wing creators into the White House. Former press secretary Karoline Leavitt opened formal press briefings to digital creators last year and hosted influencer-specific briefings during her tenure. (Leavitt’s move garnered hefty criticism for favoring softball questions, especially as the administration cracked down on the presence of more traditional media in press briefings.)

Pearson, who previously served on the RNC’s Youth Advisory Council, has been directly involved in these efforts, and points out that multiple federal agencies are now copying the White House’s playbook. He said that it’s simply the cost of getting people’s attention in the digital age.

“It’s a very chronically online administration,” he said. “With how short people’s attention spans are, you have to make [things] interesting.” Not all of the White House’s efforts have been received smoothly on the right: Pearson believes the administration’s decision to distribute portions of the Epstein files to 15 right-wing creators last year was “a misstep.”

For Abughazaleh, a former Media Matters reporter, the administration’s embrace of the internet signals something far more nefarious. Abughazaleh brought up the Department of Education’s recent use of the “Malfoid” meme—a gender-bent Harry Potter fascist figurehead—which, she said, was being promoted for a too-online audience of “incels.” And the White House’s embrace of “AI slop and brutality,” seen in ads about ICE actions, was turning “cruelty into content” rather than informing people, she says.

“They’re trying to ruin your attention span,” she said.

Abughazaleh and Pearson are pretty far apart on the political spectrum. The WIRED forum included some testy exchanges, including whether or not ICE facilities can be called “concentration camps”—a term Abughazaleh, who was arrested for protesting outside an ICE facility last year, used consistently. (The charges were dropped after allegations of misconduct by prosecutors before a grand jury.) They also skirmished over Pearson’s claim that Elon Musk had turned X into a “bastion of free speech.”

But both agreed that reliance on algorithms is increasingly creating echo chambers with young Americans of both parties.

“Algorithms show us what you want to see,” Pearson said, mentioning that his friends on the left will sometimes send him articles on political topics he simply hasn’t seen before. “The deeper question is, is that good for society or for democracy?”

On this, Abughazaleh agrees. “We have no shared reality,” she added.

ChatGPT AI Predicts: Will Crypto Recover? How Low Could We Go?

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

Bitcoin (BTC) is near $77,000 and Ethereum (ETH) nears $2,260, about $1,342 below where ETH USD price was a year ago. Bitcoin’s peak of roughly $126,000 is about 60% higher than it is now. ChatGPT AI predicts that crypto won’t just come back, but will soar to new highs.

However, the Sam Altman AI bot believes that this move will take time and may come later than the bulls want. Details and the final number are listed below, giving a solid answer to the ongoing ‘Will Crypto Recover’ question from investors.

This optimistic prediction from ChatGPT comes as the crypto market has bounced strongly from the lows after the selloff following the Democrats’ win over the Republicans in the US Midterms.

Bitcoin is trading around $78,600, up +2% today, while ETH USD sits at $2,330, up +2.5% over the same period, with both digital assets leading the market-wide rebound.

Bitcoin has been building a higher-low structure. It tested the $60,000 area in June, when traders watched the round number as price lost control, and has since recovered to the high $70,000s.

The $81,000 area remains a significant hurdle, and a weekly close above it would be my first confirmation that the base is in. A break below the $60,000 June low would invalidate the bullish case.

Ethereum looks weaker. It fell 16.1% in October and sits about 1.4% below its 200-day EMA at $2,315, with support near $2,222 and $2,170. ETH is lagging Bitcoin, which is typical early in a recovery.

A daily close above $2,315, followed by a move through $2,500, would show relative strength returning. The ETH BTC ratio is the thing to watch. A bull market needs it to turn up, because altcoin leadership is what defines a “full-blown” one.

Macro: The biggest headwind is also the biggest potential tailwind. The Fed’s September projections put the median year-end rate at 4.1%, and the 10-year yield touched 5.20%.

The Fed has been hawkish, so any pivot toward easing would unleash pent-up demand for risk assets. Bitcoin has been shrugging off some macro pressure, which suggests sellers are getting exhausted.

Geopolitics and fiscal stress: Heightened Middle East tensions and high government debt loads strengthen the “hard asset” argument for Bitcoin, much as they do for gold, especially if bond markets wobble.fxstreet

Regulation: The CLARITY Act’s Senate procedural vote failed 49–50 in September, but its failure doesn’t mean US crypto regulation has stopped moving.

Passage in a revised form would be a major institutional unlock, and one analyst argued a surprise passage could trigger a rally into the high $70,000s. Bitcoin is already there, so the pent-up reaction may be larger now.

Crypto-native: Spot ETF flows remain the marginal buyer, and cumulative inflows stayed elevated despite choppy months. Stablecoin growth also favors Ethereum, since Citi projects the stablecoin market could reach $4 trillion by 2030, much of it settling on Ethereum and its layer 2s.

Historically, post-halving cycles peak about 18 months after the halving, which points to October 2025 as the top. If that pattern holds, the real bottom comes around late 2026 or early 2027, followed by a long grind. I’m betting that ETFs, regulation, and institutional demand break the four-year rhythm, but my confidence is moderate. A prolonged rate-hike cycle is the biggest risk to this view.

Many Women Over 40 Avoid Jumping Workouts—But This Simple Exercise Is the Key to Stronger Bones

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

And you only need a small dose of plyo to reap the benefits.

THREE YEARS AGO, at 52, Tina Tang could deadlift 310 pounds without blinking and squat 210 like it was part of her warm-up. She had discovered powerlifting in her early 40s, and by all appearances, she was the picture of strength.

So when her annual physical revealed osteopenia—low bone density—she was stunned.

As a coach who trains women over 40, Tang believed she was doing everything right for her bones: lifting heavy, paying close attention to her form, eating enough protein to support muscle maintenance and growth. But one critical piece of the bone-health puzzle was missing from her routine, she would come to figure out: plyometrics.

Many women, particularly those over 40, have been told (or assume) that jumping is off-limits as they age. “The misconception is fueled by claims of increased joint pain, pelvic floor leakage, and the idea that bones become too fragile to tolerate impact,” says board-certified orthopedic surgeon, Pamela Mehta, MD, founder of Resilience Orthopedics in Los Gatos, California.

The truth: For most otherwise healthy women, plyometrics—and jumping and weight-bearing impact exercises more broadly—are among the most effective ways to protect bone health long term, says Dr. Mehta. And beyond just helping prevent bone loss, plyometric training can actually help reverse declines, emerging data suggests.

A recent review published in Current Osteoporosis Reports found plyometrics are among the most effective tools for improving bone density and reducing fracture risk in post-menopausal women. Meanwhile, a 2025 review in BMC Musculoskeletal Disorders found that when already-active people added plyometrics to their routine, they saw promising improvements in bone mineral density at the spine and hip.

Tang is living proof of these findings. At 54 years old, and just two years after adding plyometrics to her workout routine, Tang got a follow-up DEXA scan (the gold-standard test for assessing bone density) that revealed measurable improvements, including in her right hip, the area that had taken the biggest hit, per her initial scan.

“The trend is for women to continuously lose more and more bone as they age,” says Tang. Once you develop osteoporosis or osteopenia, only a small percentage of people can regain density. “So the fact that I improved mine—and plyometrics was the main thing I changed—is major,” she says.

So even though plyometrics may have gotten a bad rap among the 40-plus crowd, that reputation is rooted in nothing more than outdated pseudoscience and fear-mongering. These explosive, higher-impact exercises deliver the exact type of loading that spurs bone growth, development, and increased density.

In sum, stronger bones, better balance, faster reaction time, and a body that’s more resilient as you age are just a skip, hop, and (broad) jump away. Here's what to know—and what to do.

“Plyometrics are a category of exercises that require you to produce a tremendous amount of force in a short amount of time,” says strength and conditioning specialist Alena Luciani, MS, CSCS, founder of Training2xl. Often described as explosive or powerful, plyometrics are more impactful than traditional weight-bearing or high-impact exercises by design.

Consider traditional squats versus plyometric variations. Sure, goblet squats—which involve holding a weight in the front rack position as you sit to depth—can support bone density by challenging the musculoskeletal system, explains Luciani. But their higher-impact cousin, the jump squat, takes the stimulus to the next level. “You have to move faster and produce force much more quickly than you would during a goblet squat,” she says.

That rapid, high-impact loading is the exact type of stimulus that bones need to remodel and grow stronger, says Parini A. Patel, DO, an interventional pain and musculoskeletal medicine physician at Atlantic Health in New Jersey. In fact, recent research suggests that high(er) impact, more explosive training regimes (like plyometrics) provide even more bone benefits than traditional strength training.

One 2023 review published in the Journal of Sports Sciences observed that high-impact jumping exercise improved bone mineral density more than non-jumping alternatives.

Bones respond best when forces hit them quickly and sharply—like the impact of landing from a jump, explains Dr. Patel. “Plyometrics naturally provide these conditions in ways that low-impact activities cannot,” says Dr. Patel. Each time you land, sensor cells in the bones (called osteocytes) detect that burst of impact and signal the bone’s builder cells (osteoblasts) to lay down new bone, she explains. Over time, even short doses of this kind of impact can significantly improve bone strength.

Just as important as their impact on bone density, plyometrics also strengthen the systems that help prevent fractures in the first place. “Plyometrics also train and prime your central nervous system, helping to increase reaction time,” says Luciani. “Fast reaction time is significant for adults as they get older because it can be the difference between tripping, falling, and breaking a hip while walking on the sidewalk, and catching yourself so that the break never happens,” she says. This is key as hip fractures have been shown to increase the risk of future fractures drastically and are associated with higher mortality rates—especially in older women.

While plyo training has benefits for people across the gender spectrum, women are especially poised to reap the benefits. Women face significantly higher lifetime risks of osteopenia and osteoporosis by a large margin. (Eighty percent of all people with osteoporosis are women, studies suggest.)

One cause of this discrepancy? Hormones, says Dr. Mehta. Estrogen in particular has a protective impact on the bones; while the hormone is best known for its role in reproduction, estrogen supports new bone formation.

Unfortunately, that means that when estrogen levels drop during perimenopause and the menopause transition, the protective impact wanes and women start to lose bone mass—and quickly, says Dr. Mehta. (Men experience hormonal changes with age, too, but the effect of reduced testosterone levels on bone is far less dramatic.)

“Women also reach a lower peak bone mass in early adulthood compared to men, providing less skeletal reserve as age-related bone loss begins,” says Dr. Patel. In addition, women have smaller frames than men, and therefore are predisposed to have smaller bones, thinner cortices, and lower bone volume than men, she adds. With that, the natural declines associated with age and hormonal changes have more detrimental impacts on women over time.

Not to mention, because women live five to six years longer than men on average, “they spend more years in a lowest estrogen state," she says, which means the internal structure of the bones gradually weakens over time, raising the risk of fractures.

Women’s comparatively heightened risk for osteopenia and osteoporosis, broadly speaking, makes them ideal candidates for nearly any kind of bone-protective interventions. But plyometrics may be an especially powerful addition.

Consider this: The spine is the most common osteoporotic fracture site amongst women, while the hip is a close second. Breaks in either area can cause pain, as well as lead to immobility and loss of independence, which leads to other complications long-term. The good news is that both regions are highly responsive to plyometric training. “The hips and spine respond especially well because they absorb the most force when you land,” Dr. Patel says.

Importantly, health care providers emphasize that plyometrics aren’t meant to replace traditional strength training, but to complement it. Strength training supports muscle and tissue health more broadly, while plyometrics deliver the impact bones respond to particularly well, explains Dr. Patel. Together, the two types of loading work together to support the health of your entire frame—and research backs it up.

A study published in Applied Sciences last year found that participants who tacked on twice-weekly jump training to their usual exercise routine had improved lower-limb joint health in just 12 weeks. Meanwhile, a 2025 review in Current Osteoporosis Reports found that high-impact plyometric exercise increased lumbar spine bone mineral density in post-menopausal women. But, the authors note that the greatest benefits often appear in programs that combine resistance training with impact loading, reinforcing the idea that the two approaches work best together.

And those widespread myths that say plyometrics are off-limits after middle age? Hogwash. “Jumping does not cause wear-and-tear, worsen osteoarthritis, or increase fracture risk,” says Dr. Patel. “When done appropriately, plyometric drills are one of the most effective ways to stimulate bone formation and preserve bone density during midlife,” she says. The real risk arises from improper progression, inadequate landing mechanics, or unrecognized musculoskeletal conditions—not the jumping itself, she says.

No matter your age, goals, or training background, it’s wise to consult a physician before giving your movement practice a major overhaul, says Dr. Patel. A clinician can review your medical history, medications, bone density status, and any underlying conditions to determine whether impact training is appropriate for you at this time. Or, if you’ll need modifications.

If you have symptoms of pelvic floor dysfunction (e.g., urinary leakage, heaviness or pressure in the pelvis, pain during penetration, or discomfort during high-impact activities) it’s worth consulting a pelvic floor physical therapist first. Conditions like prolapse or incontinence often require some pelvic floor strengthening and coordination work before plyometrics feel safe and supportive, Dr. Patel says.

Once you’ve gotten the green light, the next step is to gradually introduce plyometrics into your workouts. For most, Luciani recommends picking just one or two plyometric movements per session, performing 8 to 12 total reps of each movement, with a minimum of 20 to 30 seconds between reps.

Perform these movements after your warm-up, but before your main strength work, when your nervous system is primed but still fresh enough to generate power safely.

That may not sound like much, but it’s the ideal dose. “You want to keep the volume really low because these movements are incredibly taxing on your tissues and nervous system,” she says. “The goal is quality, not quantity.” A smaller number of well-executed reps is far more effective (and safer!) than dozens of sloppy reps.

Workout Rx: Pick just one or two plyometric movements per session, performing 8–12 total reps of each movement, with a minimum of 20–30 seconds between reps.

1. Prep your body. Your current strength, balance, bone density, and overall fitness level should dictate exactly which plyometrics you introduce first. But Dr. Patel recommends beginning with movements that build Achilles tendon strength and lower-leg resilience, since the Achilles absorbs a large portion of the force when you land from a jump. Calf raises, heel drops, and small pogo hops can all help prep the tissues and joints for higher-impact training, she says.

As you hop, make sure to prioritize two-foot takeoffs and proper landing mechanics (soft knees, send back hips, engaged core), says Dr. Patel. This will help distribute impact throughout the lower body and reduce undue stress on your joints, all while still delivering the bone-building stimulus that plyometrics are known for, she explains.

Stay here for a minimum of two to three weeks, or as long as it takes for the hops to feel smooth and you to feel confident.

2. Level up to staggered-stance jumps. When you feel stable and controlled with two-foot hops, Tang recommends graduating to staggered-stance jumps. “Start with one foot just slightly in front—the change in stance forces your body to find its center of mass differently,” she explains. The mid-line strength and increased balance you gain here will make later progressions—like broad jumps and single-leg hops—much more accessible and safer. Just don’t forget to practice with both your dominant and non-dominant foot forward, as you want to build balanced strength and stability, she says.

3. Add more explosive options. After a few weeks of symptom-free, beginner-friendly plyometrics, you’re ready to try advanced plyometric exercises, such as:

These all require you to generate even more force even more quickly, which is precisely what makes them so effective for bone health—but also why they need to be approached thoughtfully, says Luciani.

5. Try unilateral plyometrics. “One of the hardest next levels is single-leg hopping,” says Tang. Unilateral plyometrics demand significantly more balance, stability, and force production from each leg—which is exactly why they’re so effective, she says. “Try hopping on a single leg in place, hopping forward, hopping left and right.” As you gain proficiency in these one-sided movements, try increasing the distance you cover with each hop,” she suggests.

6. Don’t forget about midline and upper-body plyometrics. Lower-body plyometrics may get all the attention, but if you want stronger bones or midline, you shouldn’t snooze on upper-body variations. “Upper-body plyometrics are fun to do, dynamic, great for stress relief, and often utilize a medicine ball,” says Luciani. These exercises—which include med-ball throws, d-ball slams, rotational wall throws, and the like—help you turn, rotate, react, and produce power through your upper and middle body, which is a real asset as we age,” she says.

Luciani suggests one of your two to three weekly plyometric sessions, an upper-body or midline-focused workout. Ideally, hitting them before an upper-body-focused lifting day, like shoulder and biceps or back and triceps.

7. Prioritize recovery. No matter your progression, plyometrics require a lot from your body. Yep, even though you’re only supposed to do fewer than 12 reps per session. “You want to allow 48 hours between your higher-intensity plyometric sessions for proper recovery,” says Dr. Patel. You can further support recovery by prioritizing sleep, eating nutrient-dense foods and enough of them, and implementing stress-relief exercises.

Additionally, be sure to check in with your body. “Sharp pain, new neurological symptoms, pelvic floor symptoms, and prolonged soreness are signs that it’s time to pause progression and check in with an expert,” she says.

Mercedes Delays U.S. GLC Production, Leaving the Model Vulnerable to Tariffs

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The brand’s bread-and-butter crossover won’t be made in the United States until the next generation launches.

Mercedes-Benz is delaying the start of its U.S. production of the GLC-class crossover, according to Automotive News. The automaker had originally planned for production of the GLC to begin in late 2027, but the paper reports that it has now been delayed to 2030. When the U.S. production does begin, it will take place at the automaker’s Vance, Alabama, plant.

According to the AN report, Mercedes is delaying the move in production to wait for the arrival of the next-generation GLC-class. “Investing in the plant for about half a life cycle and then having to retool for the next-generation GLC doesn’t make a lot of sense financially,” Sam Fiorani, the vice president of AutoForecast, told the outlet. The Alabama plant, in its current configuration, is home to the GLS-class and GLE-class SUVs, along with their electric counterparts.

See all results for new 2027 Mercedes-Benz GLC-Class for sale near 98837

The updated timeline lines up with the expected arrival of the next-generation GLC-class; the current model received its most recent refresh in 2023. Unfortunately, the delay leaves the GLC-class, which is one of the most important models for the brand in the United States, exposed to increased costs related to import tariffs. Mercedes has previously committed to spending some $4 billion at its Alabama plant to localize the production of its high-volume vehicles, which are currently subject to 15 percent import tariffs.

On the flip side, some reports suggest that the Trump administration is considering lowering its tariff on European cars to 10 percent in an attempt to sway Audi into building a planned $4 billion Georgia plant. Car and Driver reached out to Mercedes-Benz for confirmation of the AN report but has not heard back at the time of this article’s publication. We will update this story when we learn more.

Jack Fitzgerald’s love for cars stems from his as yet unshakable addiction to Formula 1. After a brief stint as a detailer for a local dealership group in college, he knew he needed a more permanent way to drive all the new cars he couldn’t afford and decided to pursue a career in auto writing. By hounding his college professors at the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee, he was able to travel Wisconsin seeking out stories in the auto world before landing his dream job at Car and Driver. His new goal is to delay the inevitable demise of his 2010 Volkswagen Golf.

The group's unofficial tally names CLARITY backers among the winners, but its ratings do not establish votes for passage.

The group's results page also listed 106 House winners and eight Senate winners it rated “anti-crypto.” Its tally was marked updated at 9 a.m. ET and showed 384 of 435 House races and 33 of 35 Senate races called.

The group credits Decision Desk HQ for results and says they remain unofficial until certified. The pro-crypto and anti-crypto totals are also smaller than the number of called races displayed for each chamber.

Ads are being tested in the community platform's own search results with a select group of advertisers, matched partly by relevance to what people search.

Reddit is testing ads in its own search results pages with a select group of eligible advertisers.

The company announced the test in a short newsroom post on Tuesday and said it will evaluate how users respond so it can refine the experience over time. The post doesn’t say how advertisers were chosen or whether others can ask to join.

“These placements leverage existing ad targeting in search results pages on Reddit, which have high purchase and search intent.”

“Ads in search may appear based on a range of factors, including contextual relevance to a user’s search query.”

The post does not mention what else would factor into which ads appear. In the example, a person searching for “espresso station” might see ads for coffee beans, mugs, or espresso machines, and “home office setup” may lead to advertisements for desks, office chairs, or monitors.

Example screens in the post show the mobile version of the Reddit application after searching for “home office setup”. Each screen shows a card labeled “Ad” containing a picture of a product (with a website address) and a “Shop Now” button. One example shows a card sitting under a blurb citing subreddits as its sources. Another example shows a card placed between two posts.

Brooke Osmundson reported that in October 2024, Reddit extended the keyword targeting it uses in its conversation placements to the Feed of the platform.

In its first quarter shareholder letter, Reddit stated that the product placements allow users searching on the site to browse listings and shop right from within those search results. In a phone call with investors after their Q1 earnings were released (April 30), Chief Operating Officer Jennifer Wong stated that Reddit didn’t “even have ads in certain high growing surfaces like search, for example.” On this same call, CEO Steve Huffman reported that “Search WAUqs are up 30% year-over-year.” Search WAUqs is Reddit’s shorthand for weekly active uniques who search.

During a Q2 investor call, Wong referred to search as “a space that we’re eyeing” for advertising and mentioned that Reddit has the infrastructure “already queued up,” according to Roger Montti. Wong also referenced Shopping Listing Ads, a format that groups several retailers’ products in one module, as examples of the type of advertisements she believes could be used on a search page.

Beyond eligibility, Reddit’s post is also silent regarding the other key elements of how the test works, such as markets, ad format types, ad pricing models, and whether it covers more than the app used in its examples.

Reddit is pitching its search pages to advertisers on the strength of what people type there. For now, Reddit’s search ads are limited to the advertisers it selected for the test. You can ask your Reddit rep whether your account is part of the test and how search impressions would show up in reporting.

Advertisers outside the test can watch for search to appear among Reddit’s ad placements. The post gives no end date for the test and no timeline for adding advertisers.

How To Initiate Sex Again After A Long Drought, Because We've All Been There

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

It’s all about easing in (and examining what got you here.)

Work, kids, family obligations, running your household, a hobby if you’re lucky enough to have time for one — all of these things take time and energy. It’s enough to have you laid out on the couch, carcass style, at the end of a regular day. When something actually goes wrong, like the kids get sick or a work project keeps you at your desk late, it’s even harder to imagine getting it up at the end of the day. No matter how much you love your spouse, sex droughts come for everyone at some point (ahem, many points) throughout a relationship. If it’s been a while and you want to get back in the game, here’s how to reinitiate.

Even when you’ve been married for years, once you and your partner get in a routine of not having sex, it can feel awkward or strange to try and start it up again. It almost feels like you have to acknowledge the drought, and therefore the bad things that led up to it, or perhaps the parts you’ve both played in letting it drag on. It’s just a lot, but try not to blame yourself.

“Sex droughts are far more common than pop culture would have us believe. They're certainly nothing to be ashamed of, nor do they represent a ‘failure,’” says Angie Rowntree, ABS, board-certified sexologist and founder and filmmaker behind the award-winning ethical adult film site Sssh.com. “Life happens. Bodies get sick or injured, or may have different needs. Children are born and demand your time and care. Older parents may need care. Jobs might change. Any number of stresses and interruptions in your life can upend what was your regularly scheduled Saturday date night or sap you of the carefree mojo you’d usually bring to the bedroom. None of that is a shortcoming, or is anyone’s fault.”

Every couple is different in terms of how much sex is enough for them to feel happy with it, so remember that you don’t have to meet some made-up quota of intimate evenings per week. If you are ready to get back into the groove, though, here’s how to start.

First, initiating sex after a long break comes with natural stress and pressure. If it doesn’t land with your spouse, don’t let it get you down.

“Sometimes we need a little warm-up before we get back in the saddle, so to speak. Start small. Don’t hint with memes, but act. Physical touch and flirtation are things that ignite foreplay in the mind. Kiss for a little longer than usual before you leave. Send a suggestive text during the day. Get into bed and cuddle, but without immediately reaching below the belt. You’re essentially reminding each other that you still desire each other,” Rowntree says.

It’s also totally OK to address the elephant in the room, Rowntree says. Tell your partner, “I miss being close to you. Can we make some time tonight?” This way you’re giving them an invitation and creating a little anticipation all at once.

If you go for it and your partner turns you down, don’t turn a situational no into a personal one, says Rowntree.

“When they say, ‘Honey, I’m tired,’ it becomes, ‘You don’t turn me on anymore.’ The longer you’ve been together, the more history there can be attached to that moment. If one person is usually initiating sex, or usually declining it, those long-term behavior patterns can make a huge impact.”

If there’s longstanding angst in your relationship around who initiates and how often, that in and of itself can lead to more sex droughts. It’s something to have a “frank discussion” about, Rowntree advises. During that conversation, you want to figure out how to separate “I don’t want sex right now” from “I don’t want you.”

“You want to frame your communication in a way that maintains your partner’s desirability and your interest. For instance: ‘I’m exhausted tonight, but can we cuddle?’ or, ‘Why don’t I wake you up and we can take a shower together tomorrow morning before I have to go?’ The point is, you’re declining the timing, not rejecting your partner, or giving them any ambiguity about your attraction to them.”

If issues around initiating are keeping you two on a holding pattern sex-wise, again, talk about it. Find a time when you have privacy and are fully clothed so there’s no immediate pressure, and hash out the details of each other’s wants, needs, and preferences around initiating. Rowntree says you should ask each other:

“Sometimes it really is the little things that turn a ‘meh, maybe’ into a spark that goes all the way. Also, please stop feeling like you need elaborate Instagram-worthy seduction schemes. There’s no reason to overcomplicate basic human desire. Put on music and pour a drink together after work. Send a flirty text before you get home. Give them a lingering kiss in the kitchen that morning. Or just say, ‘Why don’t we go to bed early tonight? I’ve missed you.’”

The next time you use Clear to get through airport security, it might be faster than you've come to expect.

Over the course of this year, Clear has been expanding its new “e-gates” to dozens of airports across the country.

It's a more streamlined setup that allows passengers to fly through without stopping at one of its famous blue kiosks.

As of this week, Clear had deployed its new automated gates to 53 airports across the country.

By the end of the year, the tech is expected to be in place at nearly all the airports where the company offers its flagship Clear Plus service.

That's certainly good news for travelers looking to speed through Transportation Security Administration checkpoints — especially as the busy holiday travel rush looms.

In the past, travelers hoping to use Clear at TSA would walk up to the checkpoint, then peel off to get their identity verified with a quick photo. Then, a Clear staffer would escort them to the front of the TSA line.

While it's obviously a shortcut, the service hasn't been without pain points.

Sometimes, flyers would arrive to find no open kiosks, or have to wait for Clear staffers to be free to assist them.

The new e-gate setup is intended to improve the flow of passengers.

I've used the Clear e-gates at a handful of airports this year, and am typically able to walk through pretty seamlessly — often pausing for just a few seconds to get my photo taken before the gates open.

The company touts identity verification in five seconds or less under the new design (but do let us know in the comments what your experience was like).

As of this week, Clear had installed e-gates at nearly 80% of the airports it serves — and noted the "vast majority" of its network would have the e-gates by the end of this year.

At those hubs, the company said 96% passengers were getting through in five minutes or less.

These improvements should make the service an even better shortcut at more airports.

Clear is a paid service that lets passengers effectively skip the TSA line and jump right to carry-on baggage screening. It’s typically maximized in tandem with TSA PreCheck access.

Clear’s flagship airport security service (known as Clear Plus) now costs $219 for an annual membership.

But some travel cards will offset the cost. For instance, I get a statement credit of up to $219 each year for my Clear+ membership fee by carrying the American Express Platinum Card®, which has an annual fee of $895 (see rates and fees). Enrollment required; subject to auto-renewal.

Plus, three major U.S. airlines partner with Clear to offer special pricing: Delta Air Lines, United Airlines and Alaska Airlines.

TPG Tip: If you want to try Clear and sign up using TPG's special offer, you can save $40 on the first year's membership.

Even as Clear expands its e-gates at TSA checkpoints, the program has also gotten some friendly competition from the TSA's newest security shortcut called TSA PreCheck Touchless ID.

That service, which allows members to pass through security without pulling out their ID or boarding pass, is now available at dozens of airports across the country. TSA Touchless has no extra cost for PreCheck (and Global Entry) members, though you have to opt in via your airline profile.

Report: Arsenal star's agents seek bombshell transfer to Liverpool

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

We could well see one Arsenal star move to Liverpool. His agents have sought a move and made contact with the Reds.

Arsenal and Liverpool have found themselves as rivals over the last few years, even if they haven’t actually battled in a serious title race yet. The Gunners ultimately didn’t come close to stopping the Reds in 2025, while Liverpool’s efforts in 2026 were even worse.

Yet, there’s a sense that this is the title fight for the next few years. If Liverpool can sort themselves out anyway, and we wait on the outcome of Manchester City’s guilty verdict.

As a result, no one should really expect much transfer activity between the two. Even if you don’t rate one of your players, sending them to a rival who does rate them is a recipe for disaster. It usually just means you’re undervaluing your player, in fact.

But that’s not to say it’s impossible. One Arsenal star, it’s reported, has been in contact with Liverpool over a possible move.

Martin Zubimendi to Liverpool?Liverpool tried to sign Martin Zubimendi in 2024 from Real Sociedad. It was Arne Slot's first summer and the Reds felt he was the ideal player for the new head coach. Zubimendi, however, pulled out of the deal at the last minute and remained with La Real for another year. He'd then move to Arsenal in 2025, having decided to make a big move after all. That ended successfully for Zubimendi to an extent. He was a key part of their Premier League title success but Arsenal have already replaced him. The Spaniard now barely plays and the Gunners are willing to sell.

Liverpool tried to sign Martin Zubimendi in 2024 from Real Sociedad. It was Arne Slot's first summer and the Reds felt he was the ideal player for the new head coach.

Zubimendi, however, pulled out of the deal at the last minute and remained with La Real for another year. He'd then move to Arsenal in 2025, having decided to make a big move after all.

That ended successfully for Zubimendi to an extent. He was a key part of their Premier League title success but Arsenal have already replaced him. The Spaniard now barely plays and the Gunners are willing to sell.

We've already called that a mistake on their part and one that Liverpool should take advantage of. It appears the opportunity is there, too.

TeamTalk claim that Zubimendi's representatives have contacted Liverpool about a possible move. It's not really known if the Reds are willing to make an offer at this stage, nor can we say what the price tag would be, but there is a chance of it happening.

Zubimendi almost certainly moves in 2027. Liverpool almost certainly sign a midfielder, too. Whether that's a 27-year-old, though, is another matter - such a signing would go against everything they've been doing lately.

Still, we believe he's the ideal fit for this team and exactly what's needed in the middle. Honestly, if Zubimendi and Arsenal are willing, Liverpool would be silly not to bid.

The MCU’s Multiverse Saga is nearly over, but there are plenty more Marvel movies and shows on the horizon.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Marvel fatigue? Think again, especially after Spider-Man: Brand New Day made over $2.5 billion worldwide and Avengers: Doomsday is looking hot, hot, hot!

But even after the uneven Multiverse Saga ends (hopefully on a high note), Marvel Studios won’t be stopping. So, we've put together a list of all the upcoming MCU movies and shows confirmed so far.

While the MCU isn't stopping, Marvel has slowed its roll, with the powers that be returning to the “quality over quantity” approach that defined Marvel Studios’ Infinity Saga output. This means no more than three movies per year and, perhaps more importantly, severely cutting down on TV productions, with a focus on multi-season shows rather than limited series that should’ve been a movie.

It’s a time of change for both Disney and Marvel, but it seems everyone agrees the big screen reigns supreme, so expect a thinner streaming output and more relevant movies starring characters you actually know.

More will be revealed in due time, but for now, here’s what lies at the Multiverse Saga’s end and beyond...

Spoilers ahead for the Marvel Cinematic Universe so far.

Watch Marvel movies and shows on Disney+:Disney+ (With Ads): $11.99/monthDisney+ Premium (No Ads): $18.99/month or $189.99/year

Release date: December 18, 2026 | Director: Anthony & Joe Russo | Cast: Chris Hemsworth, Chris Evans, Pedro Pascal, Robert Downey Jr.

Earth’s Mightiest Heroes (from different Earths, actually) are assembling again in December 2026.

Avengers: Doomsday's cast looks ridiculously stacked, and it’ll finally realize the dream of bringing together the Avengers, the Fantastic Four, and the X-Men (Fox’s original ones, that is) on the big screen. It’s a lot to absorb, and we don’t really know what's going on, but the trailers released so far are promising.

Anthony & Joe Russo (Captain America: The Winter Soldier & Civil War, Avengers: Infinity War & Endgame) are returning to direct, so that’s a good reason to be hyped even if the duo’s post-Marvel career hasn’t been the most exciting. Stephen McFeely (one of their two go-to scribes) is also back, and Michael Waldron (Loki) is helping out as well. It’s an all-star team for the most ambitious MCU project to date, at least until it all culminates in...

Release window: March 2027 | Showrunner: Dario Scardapane | Cast: Charlie Cox, Mike Colter, Finn Jones, Krysten Ritter

Daredevil: Born Again’s debut season was far from perfect, but most viewers agreed that Season 2 was a marked improvement.

Hopefully, that energy will continue in the third and final outing, as the Defenders reunite, with Finn Jones, Krysten Ritter, and Mike Colter all returning from their Netflix shows. Another shocker is the (unofficially) confirmed return of Elodie Yung’s Elektra.

We suspect some of these characters will be transferred to movies post-Secret Wars. In any case, Born Again’s final season should be packed, especially if Marvel Television is sticking to eight or nine episodes.

Release date: December 17, 2027 | Director: Anthony & Joe Russo | Cast: Chris Hemsworth, Chris Evans, Pedro Pascal, Robert Downey Jr.

Practically nothing about the follow-up to Avengers: Doomsday has been revealed yet (though we assume the MCU’s main players will be part of the ensemble), but we know it fully starts shooting in late 2026 and is eyeing a Christmas 2027 arrival. The plan is to leave a year-long wait between the two event movies, leaving fans hanging like back in the MCU’s glory days.

The title comes from Jonathan Hickman’s Secret Wars (2015), which is probably the main source of inspiration for the upcoming big-screen event. Incursions (realities bleeding into each other) have already been set up during the Multiverse Saga, and we know Doomsday directly deals with the multiverse’s breaking point, so it’s easy to see where the larger picture is going.

No spoilers here, but prepare for a very different kind of Avengers flick if Battleworld is indeed the main setting.

Release date: May 5, 2028 | Director: Jake Schreier | Cast: Kit Connor, Sadie Sink, Adam Driver, Christopher Abbott

Whatever happens in Secret Wars (we’ve heard Marvel Studios intends to soft-reboot), Phase Seven of the MCU will kick off with a long-awaited, fresh take on the X-Men — now part of the larger Marvel universe housing all characters. Thunderbolts* director Jake Schreier will be directing, with the same writing team from that movie joining him for another Marvel adventure.

Spider-Man: Brand New Day already did some heavy lifting when it came to setting up one of its leads (Sadie Sink’s Jean Grey), but it’s unclear how the whole group will be assembled.

We know for sure Cyclops (Kit Connor), Rogue (Inde Navarrette), Emma Frost (Samara Weaving), Storm (Maya Boyd), Angel (Asa Germann), Charles Xavier (Christopher Abbott), and Mr. Sinister (Adam Driver) are confirmed to appear, but the cast should grow in the coming months.

Release date: July 28, 2028 | Director: Shawn Levy | Cast: Ryan Gosling (more to be confirmed)

Phase Seven’s second movie will be an unexpected one, but the fan reaction to its reveal was glowing: Ghost Rider, starring Ryan Gosling, with Deadpool & Wolverine’s Shawn Levy directing.

The duo worked together on Star Wars: Starfighter, and it seems it went so well that Disney and Marvel are happy with them reviving the Spirit of Vengeance as the MCU expands its dark borders.

It’s too early to even know which Ghost Rider that Gosling will be playing (Johnny Blaze or Danny Ketch) or where the plot is going, but considering Levy’s past movies, we’re expecting an adventurous tone that blends well with Ghost Rider’s very striking, hellish visuals and darker themes.

Of course, this project is an invitation to add more magical beings to the MCU and explore the Strange-adjacent side of it. Maybe it’s our first step towards a Midnight Sons event movie? We can only hope.

Release date: December 15, 2028 | Director: Ryan Coogler | Cast: David Jonsson, Denzel Washington, Letitia Wright, Winston Duke

Ryan Coogler has taken a break from Marvel to make the excellent Sinners and help create a new iteration of The X-Files, but he’s confirmed to be coming back to wrap up his epic Black Panther trilogy. A while back, he confirmed he’d convinced Denzel Washington to join the MCU, and it sounds like those plans haven’t changed.

We also have a new T’Challa: David Jonsson (Alien: Romulus), who’s meant to play the son of the original T’Challa (Chadwick Boseman). If the years don’t make sense considering he was a kid in Wakanda Forever, wait until Secret Wars’ “soft reboot” for the answers.

What we know for sure is that series veterans Letitia Wright and Winston Duke are expected to stick around, but no plot details are available yet.

Release window: 2028 | Showrunner: Unconfirmed | Cast: Unconfirmed

Revealed at NYCC 2026, Zodiac is set to be Marvel Television’s next ongoing series after Daredevil: Born Again is over. Little is known about the premise at this point, though TV boss Brad Winderbaum has confirmed it’s a “globetrotting, international espionage show about secrets and lies and betrayals” with a packed cast of characters.

Word is the show is, more specifically, a British spy thriller with three leads, two male and one female. It’s expected to shoot in London during spring 2027, and chances are we’ll know much more about it well before that.

Of course, longtime Marvel TV fans are hoping for an Agents of SHIELD revival of sorts (its canonicity was never entirely clear), but it seems this show isn’t exactly that, though Clark Gregg (Agent Coulson) might be involved.

We’ve learned to never say never when it comes to Marvel comebacks, so stay tuned for more news.

Release windows: October 2027 / October 2028 / October 2029 | Showrunner: Bryan Andrews | Cast: Unconfirmed

Following a four-episode season in 2025, the animated series Marvel Zombies will be returning for three consecutive years (starting in 2027) just in time for Halloween.

The twist is that we’re now looking at three two-part specials: The Winter Soldier, The Fist of Khonshu, and The Last Guardian. It’s a huge departure from the previous plans with the Zombies and What If...? series, but it's one that could pay off.

Nothing is known about these small-scale projects at this point, but we know Marvel Zombies was a large enough hit on Disney+ to warrant more, and we’re okay with being surprised when it comes to format.

One has to assume these specials could work as one-shot comic books, but it’ll be interesting to see whether Marvel Television can tell cohesive and compelling stories with even shorter story arcs.

Final chance to buy Dezeen Awards 2026 party tickets

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ticket sales for this year’s Dezeen Awards party, taking place on 16 November at the Royal Opera House in London, will close next week.

Tickets are available until 23:59 London time on 10 November, meaning there are just a few more days left to secure your place.

Book your tickets now before sales close.

An evening celebrating the world’s best architecture and design

Taking place at the Royal Opera House in London on 16 November, the party will bring together shortlisted studios, judges and the international architecture and design community to celebrate this year’s projects and reveal the 2026 winners.

Guests will begin the evening with a champagne reception provided by this year’s exclusive drinks partner, Veuve Clicquot.

The champagne house will serve its signature cuvée, recognisable by its distinctive yellow labels, which have become synonymous with the brand since 1877.

Earlier this year, the brand partnered with British-Nigerian designer Yinka Ilori to create a colourful installation at Milan design week to to mark the launch of his Chasing the Sun gift collection.

Later in the evening, British architect and 2026 judge Norman Foster will deliver a keynote speech that will focus on the future of architecture, drawing on his position as one of the most influential architects of his generation.

Guests will then watch the highly anticipated announcement of this year’s Dezeen Awards winners, with 392 shortlisted projects competing in 49 categories across architecture, interiors and design.

The winners have been selected by this year’s panel of industry-leading judges, following last week’s announcement of the separate public vote, in which readers selected their favourite projects to win.

Category winners announced on the evening will then go on to compete for the prestigious overall project of the year titles in architecture, interiors and design.

Tickets for the party are available at £349 per ticket. Studios that have entered this year’s Dezeen Awards are be eligible to purchase discounted tickets for £249, saving £100 per ticket.

Hundreds of architects and designers from across the globe including Japan, South Africa and the United States have already secured their tickets.

Tickets are available until 23:59 London time on 10 November. Secure your place now to avoid disappointment.

Dezeen Awards 2026 in partnership with TrimbleDezeen Awards is the ultimate accolade for architects and designers worldwide. Avianca LifeMiles → Star Alliance Premium Cabin

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →
